<li><a href="/wiki/1989_murders_of_Jesuits_in_El_Salvador" title="1989 murders of Jesuits in El Salvador">1989 murders of Jesuits</a></li></ul></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title"><a href="/wiki/Nigeria" title="Nigeria">Nigeria</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content"><a href="/wiki/Religious_violence_in_Nigeria" title="Religious violence in Nigeria">Religious violence in Nigeria</a></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title"><a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title"><a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content hlist">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title"><a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/2021_Canadian_church_burnings" title="2021 Canadian church burnings">2021 Canadian church burnings</a></li></ul></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below">
<span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:046CupolaSPietro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/046CupolaSPietro.jpg/16px-046CupolaSPietro.jpg" decoding="async" width="16" height="12" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/046CupolaSPietro.jpg/24px-046CupolaSPietro.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/046CupolaSPietro.jpg/32px-046CupolaSPietro.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a></span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Portal:Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Portal:Catholicism">Catholicism&#32;portal</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Persecutions_of_the_Catholic_Church_sidebar" title="Template:Persecutions of the Catholic Church sidebar"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Persecutions_of_the_Catholic_Church_sidebar" title="Template talk:Persecutions of the Catholic Church sidebar"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Persecutions_of_the_Catholic_Church_sidebar&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Maximilian Maria Kolbe</b> <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:85%; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Conventual" title="Order of Friars Minor Conventual">OFMConv</a></span> (born <b>Raymund Kolbe</b>; <a href="/wiki/Polish_language" title="Polish language">Polish</a>: <i lang="pl">Maksymilian Maria Kolbe</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> 1894–1941) was a Polish <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> priest and <a href="/wiki/Conventual_Franciscans" class="mw-redirect" title="Conventual Franciscans">Conventual Franciscan</a> <a href="/wiki/Friar" title="Friar">friar</a> who volunteered to die in place of a man named <a href="/wiki/Franciszek_Gajowniczek" title="Franciszek Gajowniczek">Franciszek Gajowniczek</a> in the German <a href="/wiki/Extermination_camp" title="Extermination camp">death camp</a> of <a href="/wiki/Auschwitz_concentration_camp" title="Auschwitz concentration camp">Auschwitz</a>, located in <a href="/wiki/Occupation_of_Poland_(1939%E2%80%931945)" title="Occupation of Poland (1939–1945)">German-occupied Poland</a> during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>. He had been active in promoting the veneration of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate</a> <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Virgin Mary</a>, founding and supervising the monastery of <a href="/wiki/Niepokalan%C3%B3w" title="Niepokalanów">Niepokalanów</a> near <a href="/wiki/Warsaw" title="Warsaw">Warsaw</a>, operating an <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur-radio</a> station (SP3RN), and founding or running several other organizations and publications.
</p><p>On 10 October 1982, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> Kolbe and declared him a <a href="/wiki/Martyr_of_charity" title="Martyr of charity">martyr of charity</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> venerates him as the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of amateur radio operators, drug addicts, <a href="/wiki/Political_prisoner" title="Political prisoner">political prisoners</a>, families, journalists, and prisoners.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> John Paul II declared him "the patron of our difficult century".<sup id="cite_ref-Holy_Mass_at_the_Brzezinka_Concentration_Camp_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Holy_Mass_at_the_Brzezinka_Concentration_Camp-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> His feast day is 14 August, the day of his <a href="/wiki/Martyrdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Martyrdom">martyrdom</a>.
</p><p>Due to Kolbe's efforts to promote <a href="/wiki/Consecration_and_entrustment_to_Mary" title="Consecration and entrustment to Mary">consecration and entrustment to Mary</a>, he is known as the <i>Apostle of Consecration to Mary.</i><sup id="cite_ref-ArmstrongPeterson2010-51_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ArmstrongPeterson2010-51-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Raymund Kolbe was born on 8 January 1894 in <a href="/wiki/Zdu%C5%84ska_Wola" title="Zduńska Wola">Zduńska Wola</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/Congress_Poland" title="Congress Poland">Kingdom of Poland</a>, which was then part of the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a>. He was the second son of <a href="/wiki/Weaver_(occupation)" class="mw-redirect" title="Weaver (occupation)">weaver</a> Julius Kolbe and <a href="/wiki/Midwifery" title="Midwifery">midwife</a> Maria Dąbrowska.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> His father was an <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_German" class="mw-redirect" title="Ethnic German">ethnic German</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Strzelecka1984_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Strzelecka1984-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> and his mother was <a href="/wiki/Polish_people" title="Polish people">Polish</a>. He had four brothers. Shortly after his birth, his family moved to <a href="/wiki/Pabianice" title="Pabianice">Pabianice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kolbe's life was strongly influenced in 1906, when he was 12, by a vision of the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Virgin Mary">Virgin Mary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He later described this incident:
</p>
<blockquote><p>That night I asked the Mother of God what was to become of me. Then she came to me holding two crowns, one white, the other red. She asked me if I was willing to accept either of these crowns. The white one meant that I should persevere in purity and the red that I should become a martyr. I said that I would accept them both.<sup id="cite_ref-ArmstrongPeterson2010-50_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ArmstrongPeterson2010-50-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Franciscan_friar">Franciscan friar</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Franciscan friar">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1907 Kolbe and his elder brother Francis joined the <a href="/wiki/Conventual_Franciscans" class="mw-redirect" title="Conventual Franciscans">Conventual Franciscans</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-pages_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-pages-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> They enrolled at the Conventual Franciscan minor seminary in <a href="/wiki/Lviv" title="Lviv">Lwow</a> later that year. In 1910, Kolbe was allowed to enter the <a href="/wiki/Novitiate" title="Novitiate">novitiate</a>, where he chose a religious name Maximilian. He professed his <a href="/wiki/First_vows" class="mw-redirect" title="First vows">first vows</a> in 1911, and <a href="/wiki/Final_vows" class="mw-redirect" title="Final vows">final vows</a> in 1914,<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> adopting the additional name of Maria (Mary).<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_I">World War I</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: World War I">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kolbe was sent to <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> in 1912, where he attended the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Gregorian_University" title="Pontifical Gregorian University">Pontifical Gregorian University</a>. He earned a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_philosophy" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor of philosophy">doctorate in philosophy</a> in 1915 there. From 1915 he continued his studies at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_St._Bonaventure" title="Pontifical University of St. Bonaventure">Pontifical University of St. Bonaventure</a>, where he earned a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_theology" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor of theology">doctorate in theology</a> in 1919<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> or 1922<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> (sources vary). He was active in the <a href="/wiki/Consecration_and_entrustment_to_Mary" title="Consecration and entrustment to Mary">consecration and entrustment to Mary</a>.
</p><p>In the midst of these studies, <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a> broke out. Maximilian's father, Julius Kolbe, joined <a href="/wiki/Jozef_Pi%C5%82sudski" class="mw-redirect" title="Jozef Piłsudski">Jozef Piłsudski's</a> <a href="/wiki/Polish_Legions_in_World_War_I" title="Polish Legions in World War I">Polish Legions</a> fighting against the Russians for an independent Poland, still subjugated and still <a href="/wiki/Partitions_of_Poland" title="Partitions of Poland">divided among Prussia, Russia, and Austria</a>. Julius Kolbe was caught and hanged as a traitor by the Russians at the relatively young age of 43, a traumatic event for young Maximilian.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During his time as a student, he witnessed vehement demonstrations against Popes <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pius X</a> and <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XV" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XV">Benedict XV</a> in Rome during an anniversary celebration by the <a href="/wiki/Freemasons" class="mw-redirect" title="Freemasons">Freemasons</a>. According to Kolbe:
</p>
<blockquote><p>They placed the black standard of the "<a href="/wiki/Giordano_Bruno" title="Giordano Bruno">Giordano Brunisti</a>" under the windows of the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican</a>. On this standard the archangel, <a href="/wiki/Michael_(archangel)" title="Michael (archangel)">Michael</a>, was depicted lying under the feet of the triumphant Lucifer. At the same time, countless pamphlets were distributed to the people in which the Holy Father (i.e., the Pope) was attacked shamefully.<sup id="cite_ref-biosummary_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-biosummary-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-anniversary_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-anniversary-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Soon afterward, on 16 October 1917, Kolbe organized the <a href="/wiki/Militia_Immaculatae" title="Militia Immaculatae">Militia Immaculatae</a> (Army of the Immaculate One), to work for conversion of sinners and enemies of the Catholic Church, specifically the Freemasons, through the intercession of the Virgin Mary.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> So serious was Kolbe about this goal that he added to the Miraculous Medal prayer:
</p>
<blockquote><p>O Mary, conceived without sin, pray for us who have recourse to thee. <i>And for all those who do not have recourse to thee; especially the Freemasons and all those recommended to thee</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Daily_Prayers_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Daily_Prayers-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Kolbe wanted the entire <a href="/wiki/Franciscan_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan Order">Franciscan Order</a> consecrated to the Immaculate by an additional vow. The idea was well received, but faced the hurdles of approval by the hierarchy of the order and the lawyers, so it was never formally adopted during his life and was no longer pursued after his death.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ordination">Ordination</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Ordination">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1918, Kolbe was <a href="/wiki/Holy_Orders" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Orders">ordained</a> a priest.<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> In July 1919, he returned to <a href="/wiki/Second_Polish_Republic" title="Second Polish Republic">Poland, which was newly independent</a>. He was active in promoting the veneration of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate</a> <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Virgin Mary</a>. He was strongly opposed to <a href="/wiki/Leftist" class="mw-redirect" title="Leftist">leftist</a> – in particular, <a href="/wiki/Communist" class="mw-redirect" title="Communist">communist</a> – movements.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1919 to 1922, he taught at the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w_Seminary&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kraków Seminary (page does not exist)">Kraków Seminary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Around that time, as well as earlier in Rome, he suffered from <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, which forced him to take a lengthy leave of absence from his teaching duties. In those pre-antibiotic times, tuberculosis was generally considered fatal, with rest and good nutrition the best treatment.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 1922, Kolbe founded the monthly periodical <i><a href="/wiki/Rycerz_Niepokalanej" title="Rycerz Niepokalanej">Rycerz Niepokalanej</a></i> (<i>Knight of the Immaculata</i>), a devotional publication based on French <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Le_Messager_du_Coeur_de_Jesus&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Le Messager du Coeur de Jesus (page does not exist)">Le Messager du Coeur de Jesus</a></i> (<i>Messenger of the Heart of Jesus</i>). From 1922 to 1926, he operated a religious publishing press in <a href="/wiki/Grodno" title="Grodno">Grodno</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> As his activities grew in scope, in 1927 he founded a new Conventual Franciscan monastery at <a href="/wiki/Niepokalan%C3%B3w" title="Niepokalanów">Niepokalanów</a> near Warsaw. It became a major religious publishing centre.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> A junior seminary was opened there two years later.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Missionary_work_in_Asia">Missionary work in Asia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Missionary work in Asia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Between 1930 and 1936, Kolbe undertook a series of <a href="/wiki/Mission_(Christian)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mission (Christian)">missions</a> to <a href="/wiki/East_Asia" title="East Asia">East Asia</a>. He arrived first in <a href="/wiki/Shanghai" title="Shanghai">Shanghai</a>, <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>, but failed to gather a following there.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Next he moved to <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a>, where by 1931 he had founded a <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Conventual" title="Order of Friars Minor Conventual">Franciscan monastery</a>, <i>Mugenzai no Sono</i>, on the outskirts of <a href="/wiki/Nagasaki" title="Nagasaki">Nagasaki</a>.
</p><p>Kolbe had started publishing a Japanese edition of the <i>Knight of the Immaculata</i> (<span title="Japanese-language romanization"><i lang="ja-Latn">Seibo no Kishi</i></span>: <span title="Japanese-language text"><span lang="ja">聖母の騎士</span></span>).<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The monastery he founded remains prominent in the Roman Catholic Church in Japan.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Kolbe had the monastery built on a mountainside. According to <a href="/wiki/Shinto" title="Shinto">Shinto</a> beliefs, this was not the side best suited to be in harmony with nature. However, when the United States dropped the <a href="/wiki/Atomic_bombing_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki" class="mw-redirect" title="Atomic bombing of Hiroshima and Nagasaki">atomic bomb on Nagasaki</a>, the Franciscan monastery survived, unlike the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception_Cathedral,_Nagasaki" title="Immaculate Conception Cathedral, Nagasaki">Immaculate Conception Cathedral</a>, the latter having been on the side of the mountain that took the main force of the blast.<sup id="cite_ref-guardian_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-1932, Kolbe left Japan for <a href="/wiki/Malabar_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Malabar region">Malabar</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>, where he founded another monastery, which has since closed.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_to_Poland">Return to Poland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Return to Poland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Meanwhile, in his absence the monastery at Niepokalanów began to publish a daily newspaper <i>Mały Dziennik</i> (<i>the Small Diary</i>), in alliance with the political group <a href="/wiki/National_Radical_Camp_(1934)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Radical Camp (1934)">National Radical Camp</a> (<i>Obóz Narodowo Radykalny</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> This publication reached a circulation of 137,000, and nearly double that, 225,000, on weekends.<sup id="cite_ref-Media_katolickie_w_III_Rzeczypospolitej_(1989–2009)_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Media_katolickie_w_III_Rzeczypospolitej_(1989–2009)-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Kolbe returned to Poland in 1933 for a general chapter of the order in <a href="/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w" title="Kraków">Kraków</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Kolbe returned to Japan and remained there until called back to attend the Provincial Chapter in Poland in 1936. There he was appointed guardian of Niepokalanów, thus precluding his return to Japan. Two years later, in 1938, he started a radio station at Niepokalanów, <i>Radio Niepokalanów</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-Historia_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Historia-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He held an <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur radio</a> licence, with the call sign SP3RN.<sup id="cite_ref-qrz_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-qrz-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II">World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>After the outbreak of <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, Kolbe was one of the few friars who remained in the monastery, where he organized a temporary hospital.<sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> After the town was captured by the Germans, they arrested him on 19 September 1939; he was later released on 8 December.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb296_6-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb296-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He refused to sign the <a href="/wiki/Volksliste" class="mw-redirect" title="Volksliste">Deutsche Volksliste</a>, which would have given him rights similar to those of German citizens in exchange for recognizing his ethnic German ancestry.<sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Upon his release he continued work at his friary where he and other friars provided shelter to refugees from <a href="/wiki/Greater_Poland" title="Greater Poland">Greater Poland</a> including 2,000 Jews whom he hid from German persecution in the Niepokalanów friary.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-guardian_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Kolbe,_Saint_of_Auschwitz_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kolbe,_Saint_of_Auschwitz-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Kolbe received permission to continue publishing religious works, though significantly reduced in scope.<sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The monastery continued to act as a publishing house, issuing a number of anti-Nazi German publications.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Auschwitz">Auschwitz</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Auschwitz">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes,_KZ_Auschwitz_I,_Block_11.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg/170px-Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="221" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg/255px-Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg/340px-Todeszelle_Pater_Maximilian_Kolbes%2C_KZ_Auschwitz_I%2C_Block_11.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1904" data-file-height="2472" /></a><figcaption>Maximilian Kolbe's prison cell in <a href="/wiki/Block_11" title="Block 11">Block 11</a>, Auschwitz concentration camp</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 17 February 1941, the monastery was shut down by the German authorities. That day Kolbe and four others were arrested by the <a href="/wiki/Gestapo" title="Gestapo">Gestapo</a> and imprisoned in the <a href="/wiki/Pawiak" title="Pawiak">Pawiak</a> prison.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> On 28 May, he was transferred to <a href="/wiki/Auschwitz" class="mw-redirect" title="Auschwitz">Auschwitz</a> as prisoner 16670.<sup id="cite_ref-Sixty-ninth_Anniversary_of_the_Death_of_St._Maximilian_Kolbe_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sixty-ninth_Anniversary_of_the_Death_of_St._Maximilian_Kolbe-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg/220px-DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="189" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg/330px-DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg/440px-DBP_1973_771_Maximilian_Kolbe.jpg 2x" data-file-width="840" data-file-height="720" /></a><figcaption>Kolbe, on a West German postage stamp, marked <a href="/wiki/Auschwitz" class="mw-redirect" title="Auschwitz">Auschwitz</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Continuing to act as a priest, Kolbe was subjected to violent harassment, including beatings and lashings. Once, he was smuggled to a prison hospital by friendly inmates.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Martyrdom">Martyrdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Martyrdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>At the end of July 1941, a prisoner escaped from the camp, prompting the deputy camp commander, <i><a href="/wiki/Schutzstaffel" title="Schutzstaffel">SS</a>-<a href="/wiki/Hauptsturmf%C3%BChrer" title="Hauptsturmführer">Hauptsturmführer</a></i> <a href="/wiki/Karl_Fritzsch" title="Karl Fritzsch">Karl Fritzsch</a>, to pick ten men to be starved to death in an underground bunker to deter further escape attempts. When one of the selected men, <a href="/wiki/Franciszek_Gajowniczek" title="Franciszek Gajowniczek">Franciszek Gajowniczek</a>, cried out, "My wife! My children!" Kolbe volunteered to take his place.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-pages_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-pages-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to an eyewitness, who was an assistant janitor at that time, in his prison cell Kolbe led the prisoners in prayer. Each time the guards checked on him, he was standing or kneeling in the middle of the cell and looking calmly at those who entered. After they had been starved and deprived of water for two weeks, only Kolbe and three others remained alive.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The guards wanted the bunker emptied, so they gave the four remaining prisoners lethal injections of <a href="/wiki/Phenol" title="Phenol">carbolic acid</a>. Kolbe is said to have raised his left arm and calmly waited for the deadly injection.<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_16-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He died on 14 August 1941. His remains were cremated on 15 August, the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">feast day</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 12 May 1955 Kolbe was recognized by the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Kolbe was declared <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">venerable</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> on 30 January 1969, <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Confessor_of_the_Faith" title="Confessor of the Faith">Confessor of the Faith</a> by the same Pope in 1971, and <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> on 10 October 1982.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-Plunka2012_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Plunka2012-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Upon canonization, the Pope declared Maximilian Kolbe as a confessor and a <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyr</a> of charity. The miracles that were used to confirm his beatification were the July 1948 cure of intestinal tuberculosis in Angela Testoni and in August 1950, the cure of calcification of the arteries/sclerosis of Francis Ranier; both attributed to Kolbe's intercession by their prayers to him.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Franciszek_Gajowniczek" title="Franciszek Gajowniczek">Franciszek Gajowniczek</a>, the man Kolbe saved at Auschwitz, survived the <a href="/wiki/Holocaust" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust">Holocaust</a> and was present as a guest at both the beatification and the canonization ceremonies.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg/220px-WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg/330px-WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg/440px-WestminsterAbbey-Martyrs.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a><figcaption>The statue of Kolbe (left) above the Great West Door of Westminster Abbey</figcaption></figure>
<p>After his canonisation, a feast day for Maximilian Kolbe was added to the <a href="/wiki/General_Roman_Calendar" title="General Roman Calendar">General Roman Calendar</a>. He is one of ten 20th-century martyrs who are depicted in statues above the Great West Door of <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a> <a href="/wiki/Westminster_Abbey" title="Westminster Abbey">Westminster Abbey</a>, London.<sup id="cite_ref-westminster_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-westminster-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Maximilian Kolbe is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Commemoration_(observance)" class="mw-redirect" title="Commemoration (observance)">commemoration</a> on 14 August.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Controversies">Controversies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Controversies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kolbe's recognition as a <a href="/wiki/Christian_martyrs" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian martyrs">Christian martyr</a> generated some controversy within the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-peterson_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-peterson-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> While his self-sacrifice at Auschwitz was considered saintly and heroic, he was not killed out of <i>odium fidei</i> (hatred of the faith), but as the result of his act of <a href="/wiki/Charity_(virtue)" class="mw-redirect" title="Charity (virtue)">Christian charity</a> toward another man. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> recognized this distinction at Kolbe's beatification, naming him a Confessor and giving him the unofficial title "martyr of charity." Pope John Paul II, however, overruled the commission he had established (which agreed with the earlier assessment of heroic charity). John Paul II wanted to make the point that the Nazis' systematic hatred of whole categories of humanity was inherently also a hatred of religious (Christian) faith; he said that Kolbe's death equated to earlier examples of religious martyrdom.<sup id="cite_ref-peterson_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-peterson-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Accusations_of_antisemitism">Accusations of antisemitism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Accusations of antisemitism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Kolbe has been accused of <a href="/wiki/Antisemitism" title="Antisemitism">antisemitism</a>. In 1926, in the first issue of the monthly <i>Knight of the Immaculate</i>, Kolbe said he considered Freemasons "as an organized clique of fanatical Jews, who want to destroy the church."<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> In a 1924 column, he cited the <i><a href="/wiki/Protocols_of_the_Elders_of_Zion" class="mw-redirect" title="Protocols of the Elders of Zion">Protocols of the Elders of Zion</a></i> as an "important proof" that "the founders of Zionism intended, in fact, the subjugation of the entire world", but that "not even all Jews know this".<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> In a calendar that the publishing house of his organization, the Militia of the Immaculate, published in an edition of a million in 1939, Kolbe wrote, "Atheistic Communism seems to rage ever more wildly. Its origin can easily be located in that criminal mafia that calls itself Freemasonry, and the hand that is guiding all that toward a clear goal is international Zionism. Which should not be taken to mean that even among Jews one cannot find good people."<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Newspapers he published printed articles about topics such as a <a href="/wiki/Zionism" title="Zionism">Zionist</a> plot for world domination.<sup id="cite_ref-Dershowitz1992_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dershowitz1992-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-jta_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jta-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Michael2008_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michael2008-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Slovenian philosopher <a href="/wiki/Slavoj_%C5%BDi%C5%BEek" title="Slavoj Žižek">Slavoj Žižek</a> criticized Kolbe's activities as "writing and organizing mass propaganda for the Catholic Church, with a clear anti-Semitic and anti-Masonic edge."<sup id="cite_ref-jta_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jta-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Zizek2012_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Zizek2012-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> However, a number of writers pointed out that the "Jewish question played a very minor role in Kolbe's thought and work."<sup id="cite_ref-jta_35-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jta-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BeckyReady_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BeckyReady-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> On those grounds allegations of Kolbe's antisemitism have been denounced by <a href="/wiki/Holocaust_studies" title="Holocaust studies">Holocaust scholars</a> Daniel L. Schlafly Jr. and Warren Green among others.<sup id="cite_ref-jta_35-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jta-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Kolbe's alleged antisemitism was a source of controversy in the 1980s in the aftermath of his <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Yallop2012_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Yallop2012-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During World War II, Kolbe's monastery at Niepokalanów <a href="/wiki/Rescue_of_Jews_by_Poles_during_the_Holocaust" title="Rescue of Jews by Poles during the Holocaust">sheltered Jewish refugees</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-jta_35-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jta-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> According to the testimony of a local, "When Jews came to me asking for a piece of bread, I asked Father Maximilian if I could give it to them in good conscience, and he answered me, 'Yes, it is necessary to do this because all men are our brothers.'"<sup id="cite_ref-BeckyReady_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BeckyReady-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Relic#Classifications_and_prohibitions_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Relic">First-class relics</a> of Kolbe exist, in the form of hairs from his head and beard, preserved without his knowledge by two friars at Niepokalanów who served as barbers in his friary between 1930 and 1941. Since his <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> in 1971, more than 1,000 such relics have been distributed around the world for public veneration. Second-class relics, such as his personal effects, clothing and liturgical <a href="/wiki/Vestment" title="Vestment">vestments</a>, are preserved in his monastery cell and in a chapel at Niepokalanów, where they may be venerated by visitors.<sup id="cite_ref-relics_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-relics-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Influence">Influence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Influence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Ko%C5%9Bci%C3%B3%C5%82_MB_Ostrobramskiej_w_Chrzanowie_13_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="1280" /></a><figcaption>The first monument to Maximilian Kolbe in Poland in <a href="/wiki/Chrzan%C3%B3w" title="Chrzanów">Chrzanów</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Kolbe influenced his own Order of Conventual Franciscan friars, as the <a href="/wiki/Militia_Immaculatae" title="Militia Immaculatae">Militia Immaculatae</a> movement had continued.<sup id="cite_ref-Publishing2013_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Publishing2013-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In recent years new religious and <a href="/wiki/Secular_institute" title="Secular institute">secular institutes</a> have been founded, inspired from this spiritual way. Among these are the Missionaries of the Immaculate Mary – Fr. Kolbe, the Franciscan Friars of Mary Immaculate, and a parallel congregation of <a href="/wiki/Religious_Sister" class="mw-redirect" title="Religious Sister">religious sisters</a> and others. The Franciscan Friars of Mary Immaculate are taught basic Polish so they can sing the traditional hymns sung by Kolbe, in his native tongue.<sup id="cite_ref-OFMI_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OFMI-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the friars:
</p>
<blockquote><p>Our patron, St. Maximilian Kolbe, inspires us with his unique <a href="/wiki/Mariology" title="Mariology">Mariology</a> and apostolic mission, which is to bring all souls to the Sacred Heart of Christ through the Immaculate Heart of Mary, Christ's most pure, efficient, and holy instrument of evangelization – especially those most estranged from the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-OFMI_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OFMI-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG/220px-Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG/330px-Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG/440px-Plum_Edith_Stein_und_Maximilian_Kolbe.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a><figcaption>Stained-glass window by <a href="/wiki/Alois_Plum" title="Alois Plum">Alois Plum</a> depicting <a href="/wiki/Edith_Stein" title="Edith Stein">Edith Stein</a> and Maximilian Kolbe</figcaption></figure>
<p>Kolbe's views into <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Mariology" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Mariology">Marian theology</a> echo today through their influence on <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_3-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="This reference citation appears to be to a self-published source. (October 2022)">self-published source</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> His image may be found in churches across Europe<sup id="cite_ref-westminster_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-westminster-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and throughout the world. Several churches in Poland are under his patronage, such as the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Sanctuary_of_Saint_Maxymilian&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Sanctuary of Saint Maxymilian (page does not exist)">Sanctuary of Saint Maxymilian</a> in <a href="/wiki/Zdu%C5%84ska_Wola" title="Zduńska Wola">Zduńska Wola</a> or the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Church_of_Saint_Maxymilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Church of Saint Maxymilian Kolbe (page does not exist)">Church of Saint Maxymilian Kolbe</a> in <a href="/wiki/Szczecin" title="Szczecin">Szczecin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sanktuarium_Św._Maksymiliana_–_Zduńska_Wola_–_DIECEZJA_WŁOCŁAWSKA_-KURIA_DIECEZJALNA_WŁOCŁAWSKA_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sanktuarium_Św._Maksymiliana_–_Zduńska_Wola_–_DIECEZJA_WŁOCŁAWSKA_-KURIA_DIECEZJALNA_WŁOCŁAWSKA-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-smmkolbeci_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-smmkolbeci-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> A museum, <a href="/wiki/Museum_of_St._Maximilian_Kolbe_%22There_was_a_Man%22" title="Museum of St. Maximilian Kolbe &quot;There was a Man&quot;">Museum of St. Maximilian Kolbe "There was a Man"</a>, was opened in Niepokalanów in 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-Niepokalanów_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Niepokalanów-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1963, <a href="/wiki/Rolf_Hochhuth" title="Rolf Hochhuth">Rolf Hochhuth</a> published <i><a href="/wiki/The_Deputy" title="The Deputy">The Deputy</a></i>, a play significantly influenced by Kolbe's life and dedicated to him.<sup id="cite_ref-psb297_23-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-psb297-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> In 2000, the National Conference of Catholic Bishops (US) designated Marytown, home to a community of Conventual Franciscan friars, as the <a href="/wiki/National_Shrine" class="mw-redirect" title="National Shrine">National Shrine</a> of St. Maximilian Kolbe. Marytown is located in <a href="/wiki/Libertyville,_Illinois" title="Libertyville, Illinois">Libertyville, Illinois</a>. It features the Kolbe Holocaust Exhibit.<sup id="cite_ref-National_Shrine_of_St._Maximilian_Kolbe_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-National_Shrine_of_St._Maximilian_Kolbe-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> In 1991, <a href="/wiki/Krzysztof_Zanussi" title="Krzysztof Zanussi">Krzysztof Zanussi</a> released a Polish film about the life of Kolbe, <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Life_for_Life:_Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Life for Life: Maximilian Kolbe (page does not exist)">Life for Life: Maximilian Kolbe</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BBycie_za_%C5%BCycie._Maksymilian_Kolbe" class="extiw" title="pl:Życie za życie. Maksymilian Kolbe">pl</a>&#93;</span></i>, in which <a href="/wiki/Edward_%C5%BBentara" title="Edward Żentara">Edward Żentara</a> played Kobe's role. The <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_Poland" title="Senate of Poland">Polish Senate</a> declared 2011 to be the year of Maximilian Kolbe.<sup id="cite_ref-senat_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-senat-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are examples of Catholic institutions around the world adopting Kolbe as their patron saint. An example of this is <a href="/wiki/Kolbe_Catholic_College,_Rockingham" title="Kolbe Catholic College, Rockingham">Kolbe Catholic College</a> in Rockingham, Western Australia. Founded in 1989, the college is a secondary education institution that uses the motto of "courage, faith and excellence" to connect with Kolbe's charism. In 2014, to commemorate the 25th anniversary of the college, staff and students went on pilgrimage to Poland and Italy to retrace the life of Kolbe. The pilgrimage includes Auschwitz to connect with "courage", Niepokalanow to connect with "faith", and Rome to connect with "excellence". The college has returned to Europe with around 16 students and 2 or 3 faculty members again in 2016, to coincide with <a href="/wiki/World_Youth_Day" title="World Youth Day">World Youth Day</a> celebrations in Krakow, and then again in 2018.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Immaculata_prayer">Immaculata prayer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Immaculata prayer">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kolbe composed the <a href="/wiki/Immaculata_prayer" title="Immaculata prayer">Immaculata prayer</a> as a prayer of <a href="/wiki/Consecration_and_entrustment_to_Mary" title="Consecration and entrustment to Mary">consecration to the Immaculata</a>, i.e. the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">immaculately conceived</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-University_of_Dayton_Marian_prayers_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-University_of_Dayton_Marian_prayers-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Maximilian_Kolbe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://catholicism.org/maximilian-kolbe.html"><i>Saint Maximilian Kolbe</i></a>, a popular biography at Catholicism.org</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://niepokalanow.eu./">Niepokalanów in English</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.catholic.org/saints/saint.php?saint_id=370"><i>Catholic Online, St. Maximilian Kolbe</i></a>, Catholic Online.Inform-Inspire-Ignite.</li>
